# [megatron] feat: wire LoRA+ and shared-outer MoE LoRA export to the optimizer/rollout path

[Upstream PR](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7484) · state: open · merged: False

## Original PR description

### What does this PR do?

Wire LoRA+ and shared expert adapters into the Megatron backend.

- **LoRA+**: `lr_B = lora_plus_ratio * lr_A`. The new `model.lora.lora_plus_ratio` field defaults to `1.0`. The optimizer uses Bridge's per-group LR helper when available and the paired MCore optimizer API with the pinned Bridge 0.6.2 wheel.
- **Shared-outer MoE LoRA**: on the unmerged path, select the layout the rollout uses (SGLang's native shared factors, per-expert 2D, stacked 3D, or vLLM shared LoRA). HF adapter checkpoints use PEFT-loadable expanded factors.

Those needs:
- https://github.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/Megatron-Bridge/pull/5589
- https://github.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/Megatron-Bridge/pull/5588
- https://github.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/Megatron-Bridge/pull/5939
- https://github.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/Megatron-Bridge/pull/6009
- https://github.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/Megatron-Bridge/pull/6225

Bridge builds with the expert-adapter finalizer retain fused accumulation. With Bridge 0.6.2, verl disables wgrad fusion on trainable EP-replicated LoRA projections so Bridge's existing hooks sum the true gradients. The legacy shared-outer layout requires identical pipeline-stage and EP membership when the shared projection is trainable or TP/GTP-sharded, because initialization broadcasts its weight and backward sums its gradients over the stage. Frozen unsharded replicas are allowed. The Megatron LoRA guide documents the optional export APIs for each serving layout.

### Checklist Before Starting

- [X] Searched for [related LoRA+ and shared-outer PRs](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pulls?q=is%3Apr+LoRA%2B+shared+outer). This PR owns the Megatron optimizer and export wiring. The model-support PRs target different code paths.
- [X] Format the PR title as `[{modules}] {type}: {description}` (This will be checked by the CI)
  - `{modules}` include `fsdp`, `megatron`, `veomni`, `sglang`, `vllm`, `rollout`, `trainer`, `ci`, `training_utils`, `recipe`, `hardware`, `deployment`, `ray`, `worker`, `single_controller`, `misc`, `perf`, `model`, `algo`, `env`, `tool`, `ckpt`, `doc`, `data`, `cfg`, `reward`, `fully_async`, `one_step_off`
  - If this PR involves multiple modules, separate them with `,` like `[megatron, fsdp, doc]`
  - `{type}` is in `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `chore`, `test`
  - If this PR breaks any API (CLI arguments, config, function signature, etc.), add `[BREAKING]` to the beginning of the title.
  - Example: `[BREAKING][fsdp, megatron] feat: dynamic batching`

### Test

- `python -m pytest -q tests/utils/megatron/test_lora_training_on_cpu.py tests/utils/megatron/test_optimizer.py tests/workers/rollout/test_vllm_cli_args_on_cpu.py` initially passed all 54 cases on DSW with GPUs hidden; the subsequent legacy-gradient follow-up is covered below.
- After the legacy-gradient follow-up, `python -m pytest -q tests/utils/megatron/test_lora_training_on_cpu.py` passed all 19 cases on both the published Bridge 0.6.2 + MCore `core_v0.19.2` stack and the prepared DSW Bridge stack. A two-rank Gloo probe through the actual helper, native MCore linears and DDP backward hook matched every element of both `main_grad` buffers to an eager reference after each of two microbatches, for FC1 and FC2. A separate two-rank Gloo probe constructed the real legacy SharedOuter FC1/FC2 adapters: frozen full replicas (ordinary TP=2, ETP=1) were accepted, while actual frozen shards (ordinary TP=1, ETP=2) were rejected.
- All 14 pre-commit hooks passed for the changed files.

- [The 8×L20 Megatron-Bridge expert-parallel E2E job](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/actions/runs/41762050376/job/126352233982) passed on `eda09da3` (attempt 1), including fused 3D training, FP8 rollout, and LoRA GSM8K cases for Qwen3-30B-A3B-Instruct-2507. The previously failing EP LoRA case and all job steps succeeded.

### API and Usage Example

Set `actor_rollout_ref.model.lora.lora_plus_ratio=16` and `actor_rollout_ref.actor.optim.lr_warmup_init=0` for a 16× B-to-A learning rate ratio on a LoRA actor.

### Design & Code Changes

The module-level optimizer overrides preserve MCore's other parameter groups. Shared-outer exports are opt-in. AI assistance was used to review, implement, and test the compatibility fixes.

> `megatron-bridge` is pinned to `0.6.2`, but that version does not define `get_lora_plus_config_overrides`. The default `1.0` path and ordinary optimizer use remain unaffected. Update the Bridge pin and lockfile to a release that exports this helper. The general LoRA support guidance allows `0.6.2`, but it does not provide this newer helper.

> **Document the Bridge requirement for opt-in LoRA+.**
> 
> `get_megatron_optimizer` skips the helper import when `lora_plus_ratio` is falsy or `1.0`. A truthy ratio other than `1.0` imports `get_lora_plus_config_overrides`, which is absent from the declared Megatron-Bridge `0.6.2`. LoRA+ is opt-in, so the default training path remains compatible, but enabling it fails with a raw `ImportError`. Document the required Bridge release or raise a clear compatibility error for non-default ratios.

> `verl/utils/megatron/optimizer.py:229-232`
> **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/major-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Major** · **Stability & Availability** · **Quick win**
> 
> **Enabling LoRA+ fails at import with the pinned Megatron-Bridge 0.6.2.**
> 
> When `model.lora.lora_plus_ratio` is positive, finite, and not `1.0`, `get_megatron_optimizer` runs `from megatron.bridge.peft.lora import get_lora_plus_config_overrides`. The pinned `megatron-bridge==0.6.2` wheel does not define this helper in `megatron/bridge/peft/lora.py`, and no other module in that wheel defines it. The import raises `ImportError` before the optimizer is built, so the LoRA+ setting cannot run with the current pin.
> 
> Raise the `megatron-bridge` pin, `uv.lock`, and the wheelhouse artifact to a release that defines `get_lora_plus_config_overrides` in `megatron.bridge.peft.lora`. Confirm the release contents before pinning it. Until the pin changes, a non-default `lora_plus_ratio` is unusable.
>

>

## Summary by CodeRabbit

* **New Features**
  * Added configurable LoRA options for expert adapters and multimodal tower connectors across PPO trainer configurations.
  * Added LoRA+ learning-rate scaling, with validation for supported ratios and warmup settings.
  * Expanded support for exporting and serving shared-expert LoRA adapters with compatible vLLM formats.
* **Bug Fixes**
  * Improved Megatron gradient synchronization and checkpoint compatibility for supported adapter configurations.
* **Documentation**
  * Expanded guidance on LoRA+ requirements and shared-expert adapter compatibility.

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->
The changes add MoE LoRA, LoRA+, and tower-connector settings to model and trainer configurations. Megatron and vLLM paths use these settings for optimizer setup, gradient finalization, adapter export, and rollout arguments. A Megatron patch imports memory helpers only after its version guard passes.

**Changes: LoRA support**

|Layer / File(s)|Summary|
|:---|:---|
|**LoRA configuration options** <br> `verl/trainer/config/model/hf_model.yaml`, `verl/trainer/config/_generated_ppo_*_trainer.yaml`|The model configuration and generated PPO trainer configurations add MoE adapter, LoRA+ ratio, and tower-connector options for actor and critic models.|
|**LoRA+ optimizer and gradient finalization** <br> `verl/workers/engine/megatron/transformer_impl.py`, `verl/utils/megatron/optimizer.py`, `verl/utils/megatron_utils.py`, `tests/utils/megatron/test_lora_training_on_cpu.py`, `docs/advance/ppo_lora.rst`|The Megatron engine validates and passes the LoRA+ ratio to the optimizer. The optimizer creates learning-rate overrides when the ratio differs from `1.0`. Training hooks select Bridge’s expert-adapter finalizer when the required helpers are available. Otherwise, legacy handling adjusts gradient accumulation fusion and checks process-group ranks. Tests and documentation cover these settings and constraints.|
|**Expert-adapter export options** <br> `verl/workers/engine_workers.py`, `verl/workers/rollout/vllm_rollout/utils.py`, `verl/workers/engine/megatron/transformer_impl.py`, `verl/utils/checkpoint/megatron_checkpoint_manager.py`|The worker passes vLLM layout settings to the engine. The engine detects 3D MoE models and selects export options for shared outer LoRAs. The checkpoint manager requests expanded export when shared outer LoRAs are enabled.|
|**Tower-connector rollout wiring** <br> `verl/workers/rollout/vllm_rollout/vllm_async_server.py`, `tests/workers/rollout/test_vllm_cli_args_on_cpu.py`|The vLLM server manages the tower-connector LoRA argument and adds it when enabled in model configuration. The managed-argument test covers this option.|

**Megatron patch import guard**

|Layer / File(s)|Summary|
|:---|:---|
|**Guarded memory utility imports** <br> `verl/models/mcore/patch.py`|The function documents its supported Megatron and PyTorch versions and imports memory utilities only after the version check passes.|

### Sequence Diagram(s)

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
  participant ActorRolloutRefWorker
  participant MegatronEngine
  participant is_3d_moe_vllm_model
  participant vLLMModelRegistry
  ActorRolloutRefWorker->>MegatronEngine: Set vLLM layout settings
  MegatronEngine->>is_3d_moe_vllm_model: Check model architecture
  is_3d_moe_vllm_model->>vLLMModelRegistry: Resolve configured architecture
  vLLMModelRegistry-->>is_3d_moe_vllm_model: Return registered model class
  is_3d_moe_vllm_model-->>MegatronEngine: Return 3D MoE classification
  MegatronEngine->>MegatronEngine: Select adapter export options
```

<summary><strong>Security architecture risk:</strong> <strong><picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/risk-normal-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Moderate</strong> · up to <code>1fbff</code></summary>

<dd>

<!-- architecture_review_start -->
<!-- architecture_review_label
### Security Architecture Review

**Security architecture risk:** **🟡 Moderate** · up to `1fbff`
-->

Adapter export gains an unconditional dependency on an optional serving backend, which can interrupt existing alternative-backend jobs during weight synchronization. No introduced privilege escalation or trust-boundary bypass was established. External format compatibility and interrupted-update recovery remain partly unverified.

**Retained concerns**
- **Medium · reliability · inferred:** Backend-neutral adapter export now imports the vLLM rollout package unconditionally on every unmerged adapter-only export, before checking the shared-outer feature flag. That package eagerly requires vLLM, while installation and rollout selection treat serving backends as alternatives. In an otherwise supported non-vLLM deployment without that dependency, synchronization can therefore fail before reaching Bridge. The caller may already have resumed rollout memory, while offload, cache resumption, and synchronization-state completion occur only on success. This is introduced dependency coupling with a failure-containment consequence, not an established attacker-driven vulnerability.


<details>
<summary><strong>Security review details</strong></summary>
<dl>
<dd>

**Security Blast Radius**
- _inferred_ — The directly affected assets are the configured actor's optimizer/model state, its exported adapter checkpoints, and attached rollout adapter state. The inspected flow establishes job-local propagation, not a tenant-wide, credential, or infrastructure authority expansion; deployment isolation beyond that flow is not established.

**Trust Boundaries and Controls**
- _observed_ — The inspected worker initialization and weight-update dispatch boundaries remain unchanged in the full PR comparison. Serving still forwards trust_remote_code from model configuration through the existing launch path. The new shared-LoRA propagation changes export format selection rather than adding a remote entrypoint.

**Hardening Proposals**
- _proposed_ — Preserve alternative-backend isolation by keeping backend-neutral export independent of eager serving-package imports. Validate required exporter capabilities and effective serving layout before entering the mutable rollout update, so unsupported combinations fail before memory resumption or adapter replacement.

</dd>
</dl>
</details>
<!-- architecture_review_end -->

</dd>
</dl>
</details>

- HollowMan6 · comment · 2026-11-03T23:17:13Z · [source](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7484#issuecomment-6617948815)

Rechecked the review findings that GitHub placed outside the diff against 6971bc09.

- The pinned Bridge 0.6.2 really does lack the LoRA+ helper. With that wheel the optimizer now builds the same A/B learning-rate overrides through the paired Megatron-Core API, starting from MCore's standard parameter groups. The default ratio still skips all overrides. The published 0.6.2 wheel and MCore v0.19.2 passed all nine compatibility cases on CPU. The broader DSW run passed 54 cases. [Original report](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7484#pullrequestreview-5490694594) · [version follow-up](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7484#pullrequestreview-5565487258) · [documentation follow-up](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7484#pullrequestreview-5704644066) · [latest report](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7484#pullrequestreview-5758395346)
- The older vLLM images in the first review are versioned deployment snapshots. The current package declares vLLM >=0.18.0, whose CLI already implements `--enable-tower-connector-lora`. The CLI suite also checked that an engine kwarg cannot override the model-level switch. [Current package requirement](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/blob/6971bc099711b36585970d1bceed8a5034854acb/setup.py#L55) · [vLLM v0.18.0 argument definitions](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/blob/v0.18.0/vllm/engine/arg_utils.py)
- Shared-outer HF checkpoints require the Bridge `expand_shared_outer` save API so the expanded factors can be loaded by PEFT. The Megatron LoRA guide now records which released APIs support this and which extra capability the vLLM shared layout needs. [Duplicate checkpoint review](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7484#pullrequestreview-5491239717) · [Megatron LoRA guide](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/blob/6971bc099711b36585970d1bceed8a5034854acb/docs/advance/ppo_lora.rst#megatron-backend-usage-guide)
- The AST fixture named in the earlier test-diagnostics suggestion was removed from the branch. The new compatibility regression uses the optimizer API, finalizer selection, and Bridge's actual adapter classes on CPU. [Fixture review](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7484#pullrequestreview-5505776480)


- HollowMan6 · comment · 2026-11-03T23:47:46Z · [source](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7484#issuecomment-6618441754)

Confirmed the coverage gap. The test now calls Bridge’s `LoRA.transform` on a CPU linear layer and passes the resulting model and verl’s overrides to Megatron-Core’s optimizer group builder using a one-rank Gloo process group. It locates the groups by the actual A and B weight objects, checks both maximum and minimum learning rates at a 4× ratio, and checks that a separate 1-D parameter still receives zero weight decay. [Review](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7484#pullrequestreview-5760929185) · [Test change](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/commit/50d1877c9dbfdc2ef4a5c82cecdd93840cf756bd)

`python -m pytest -q tests/utils/megatron/test_lora_training_on_cpu.py` passed all nine cases on both DSW stacks with GPUs hidden: the published Bridge 0.6.2 wheel with MCore v0.19.2, and the current DSW Bridge/MCore source builds. Both exercised the compatibility branch. All 14 pre-commit hooks passed.

</details>

**Align `allow_serving_layout` with the pinned Bridge API.**

When `experts_shared_outer_loras` is enabled, this call passes `allow_serving_layout=True` to `AutoBridge.save_hf_adapter`. Bridge 0.6.2 does not declare that parameter, so the checkpoint save can raise `TypeError` before exporting the adapter. This is a `save_hf_adapter` receiver mismatch, not an `export_adapter_weights` failure.

Update the Bridge pin to a release that supports this parameter, or remove the unsupported keyword while retaining Bridge 0.6.2.

**Align the Bridge pin with the expert-gradient finalizer API.**

The project pins Bridge `v0.6.2`, which does not export either imported helper. The `ImportError` handler therefore keeps `finalize_model_grads`, so the guard never selects the expert-adapter finalizer for LoRA-enabled MoE models.

Bridge `v0.6.2` only installs a per-parameter hook that reduces the callback gradient. That hook does not reduce `main_grad` when MCore uses fused gradient accumulation. Shared expert adapter gradients can therefore remain different across EP ranks. Use a Bridge build that exports both helpers, or provide an equivalent main-gradient synchronization path instead of silently falling back.

**Use a Bridge version that supports the export kwargs.**

When `experts_shared_outer_loras=True`, the actor's normal adapter-only rollout sync passes `stack_3d_moe`, `moe_shared_loras`, or `expand_shared_outer` to `self.bridge.export_adapter_weights(...)`. Megatron-Bridge 0.6.2 accepts none of these arguments, so every affected weight update raises `TypeError` and blocks rollout synchronization. Update `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, and the wheelhouse reference to a Bridge release that supports these kwargs and remains compatible with the pinned Megatron-Core version.

For an adapter-only export, this import runs even when `experts_shared_outer_loras` is false. The imported module requires `vllm` at module load. Move the architecture lookup into the opt-in vLLM branch, and keep the default export independent of vLLM. **Propagate the resolved MoE adapter layout without importing vLLM in the Megatron actor.**

When `experts_shared_outer_loras` is enabled, `get_per_tensor_param` imports `verl.workers.rollout.vllm_rollout.utils`. That module imports vLLM at module scope, so a Megatron actor environment without vLLM raises during adapter export before `is_3d_moe_vllm_model` can return `False`.

The existing `enable_mixed_moe_lora_format` and `enable_moe_shared_loras` settings do not identify whether the rollout model uses 3D weights. Resolve that layout where vLLM is available and pass an explicit layout flag to the actor. Preserve the current rule that mixed-format LoRA uses the 2D export even for a 3D target.

@HollowMan6 Yes. Catching the exception around that import handles the module-level failure too. My earlier suggestion to avoid the import entirely was more restrictive than necessary.

Use a narrow handler so unrelated import failures remain visible:

```python
is_3d_moe = False
if not getattr(self, "vllm_enable_mixed_moe_lora_format", False):
    try:
        from verl.workers.rollout.vllm_rollout.utils import is_3d_moe_vllm_model
    except ModuleNotFoundError as exc:
        if exc.name != "vllm":
            raise
    else:
        is_3d_moe = is_3d_moe_vllm_model(self.model_config.hf_config)
```

Keep the existing layout-selection branches after this block. If vLLM is absent, `is_3d_moe` stays `False`. With the shared-vLLM flag also at its default `False`, export selects `expand_shared_outer=True`.

**🗄️ Data Integrity & Integration** | **🟠 Major** | **⚡ Quick win**

**Preserve the shared-outer layout for SGLang.**

If an unmerged shared-outer adapter targets SGLang, this fallback sets `expand_shared_outer=True`. Bridge documents that option as the vLLM 2D layout; SGLang expects the shared factor with expert dimension `1`. The worker can pass this export to SGLang’s tensor adapter loader, so the update receives incompatible adapter weights. Pass the rollout backend into this decision. Leave `export_kwargs` empty for SGLang, and select vLLM layout options only for vLLM. ([docs.nvidia.com](https://docs.nvidia.com/nemo/megatron-bridge/nightly/apidocs/bridge/bridge.models.conversion.auto_bridge.html))

**Prevent `engine_kwargs.vllm` from bypassing the model opt-out.**

`enable_tower_connector_lora` is derived from `model_config.lora`, but it is missing from `_VERL_MANAGED_VLLM_ARGS`. If the model setting is `False` and `rollout.engine_kwargs.vllm.enable_tower_connector_lora=True`, the flag remains in `args` and vLLM enables the feature despite the documented opt-out. Add this option to `_VERL_MANAGED_VLLM_ARGS`.

<details>
<summary>Proposed fix</summary>
<dl>
<dd>

```diff
 _VERL_MANAGED_VLLM_ARGS = {
     "enable_lora": "actor_rollout_ref.model.lora",
+    "enable_tower_connector_lora": "actor_rollout_ref.model.lora",
 }
```
</dd>
</dl>
</details>

**Re-raise missing-module errors that do not mean vLLM is absent.**

The handler also catches a `ModuleNotFoundError` from a missing transitive dependency of the vLLM utility import. In that case, `is_3d_moe` stays false, and the export selects the 2D layout for a 3D MoE rollout model. The rollout process imports the same utility at startup, so it fails on the same missing dependency. The wrong layout is a misleading first failure, not a silently consumed adapter.

The absent-vLLM case raises `PackageNotFoundError` from the package initializer. Its `name` does not equal `"vllm"`, so a `name` check alone would re-raise the intended fallback. Catch `PackageNotFoundError` first. Then re-raise every other `ModuleNotFoundError` whose `name` is not `"vllm"`.

<details>
<summary>Proposed change</summary>
<dl>
<dd>

```diff
--- "a/verl/workers/engine/megatron/transformer_impl.py"
+++ "b/verl/workers/engine/megatron/transformer_impl.py"
@@ -1023,8 +1023,11 @@
 
                         # Mixed-format LoRA uses vLLM's 2D wrapper even for 3D model weights.
                         is_3d_moe = is_3d_moe_vllm_model(self.model_config.hf_config)
-                    except ModuleNotFoundError:
-                        pass
+                    except PackageNotFoundError:
+                        pass
+                    except ModuleNotFoundError as exc:
+                        if exc.name != "vllm":
+                            raise
 
                 vllm_shared = getattr(self, "vllm_enable_moe_shared_loras", False)
                 if is_3d_moe:
```

Import `PackageNotFoundError` from `importlib.metadata` if the module does not already import it.

</dd>
</dl>
</details>

```
<title>model_manager - vLLM</title>
<location>https://docs.vllm.ai/en/stable/api/vllm/lora/model_manager/</location>
<excerpt>citeturn0search1 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: last week;                         f&quot;LoRA adapter {lora_model.id} contains fused 3D MoE weights &quot; ...         self._enable_moe_shared_loras = is_moe and lora_config.enable_moe_shared_loras ... Source code in `vllm/lora/model_manager.py`
                    `(rank * num_experts, hidden)` / `(intermediate * 2,
                    rank * num_experts)`
              - `{module}.lora_{A,B}`: down_proj, with shapes
                    `(rank * num_experts, intermediate)` / `(hidden,
                    rank * num_experts)`
            The 2D wrapper expects three stacked per-expert tensors,
            `[w1, w2, w3]`, with `(num_experts, rank, in)` for lora_a and
            `(num_experts, out, rank)` for lora_b. In the 3D layout w1
            (gate_proj) and w3 (up_proj) share the rank-r intermediate
            representation, so both halves use the same lora_a tensor.
    
            Only invoked when `enable_mixed_moe_lora_format=True` and the
            source LoRARequest declares `is_3d_lora_weight=True`.
            &quot;&quot;&quot;
            gate_up_proj_lora = self._get_lora_layer_weights(
                lora_model, module_name + &quot;.base_layer&quot;
            )
            down_proj_lora = self._get_lora_layer_weights(lora_model, module_name)
            if gate_up_proj_lora is None or down_proj_lora is None:
                    )
            return lora_model.get_lora(org_module_name)
    
        def _get_module_to_save_weights(
            self, lora_model: LoRAModel, module_name: str
        ) -&gt; LoRAFullModuleWeights | None:
            weights = lora_model.get_module_to_save(module_name)
            if weights is not None:
                return weights
    
            unprefixed_module_name = module_name.removeprefix(&quot;model.&quot;)
            weights = lora_model.get_module_to_save(unprefixed_module_name)
            if weights is not None and unprefixed_module_name != module_name:
                logger.info_once(
                    &quot;For the pool model, successfully loaded the full module weights &quot;
                    &quot;after removing the prefix &#39;model.&#39;.&quot;
                )
            return weights
    
        def _validate_moe_lora_format(self, lora_model: LoRAModel) -&gt; None:
            if self._enable_mixed_moe_lora_format and getattr(
                lora_model, &quot;is_3d_lora_weight&quot;, False
            ):
                return
            for module_name, module in self.modules.items():
                if not isinstance(module, FusedMoEWithLoRA) or isinstance(
                    module, FusedMoE3DWithLoRA
                ):
                    continue
                module_lora = self._get_lora_layer_weights(lora_model, module_name)
                if module_lora is not None and torch.is_tensor(module_lora.lora_a):
                    raise ValueError(
                        f&quot;LoRA adapter {lora_model.id} contains fused 3D MoE weights &quot;
                        f&quot;for {module_name!r}, but the model uses a 2D MoE LoRA &quot;
                        &quot;wrapper. Start the engine with &quot;
                        &quot;enable_mixed_moe_lora_format=True &quot;
                        &quot;(--enable-mixed-moe-lora-format) and set &quot;
                        &quot;is_3d_lora_weight=True on the LoRA request.&quot;
                    )
    
        def _validate_modules_to_save(self, lora_model: LoRAModel) -&gt; None:
            if not lora_model.modules_to_save:
                return
            saved_module_name = next(iter(lora_model.modules_to_save))
            if self._classification_head is None:
                raise ValueError(
                    f&quot;Cannot load full module {saved_module_name!r}: the model does &quot;
                    &quot;not expose a unique ClassificationHeadWithLoRA.&quot;
                )
    
            module_name, wrapper = self._classification_head
            full_module = self._get_module_to_save_weights(lora_model, module_name)
        self.modules: dict[str, BaseLayerWithLoRA] = {}
        self._last_mapping: LoRAMapping | None = None
        self._last_slot_layout: tuple[int | None, ...] | None = None
        is_moe = is_moe_model(self.model)
        self._is_moe = is_moe
    
        self.supported_modules_to_save = (
            {&quot;score&quot;, &quot;classifier&quot;}
            if self.is_pooling_model
            and vllm_config.model_config.score_type == &quot;cross-encoder&quot;
            else set()
        )
    
        # Mixed-format and shared-outer adapters both use FusedMoEWithLoRA.
        # FusedMoE3DWithLoRA only implements the fused gate/up PEFT pair.
        self._enable_mixed_moe_lora_format = (
            is_moe and lora_config.enable_mixed_moe_lora_format
        )
        self._enable_moe_shared_loras = is_moe and lora_config.enable_moe_shared_loras
        self._is_3d_moe_model = (
            self._is_moe
            and self.model.is_3d_moe_weight
            and not self._enable_mixed_moe_lora_format
            and not self._enable_moe_shared_loras
        )
        self.packed_modules_mapping = process_packed_modules_mapping(
            self.model,
            force_2d_moe=self._enable_mixed_moe_lora_format,
            enable_moe_shared_loras=self._enable_moe_shared_loras,
        )
        self._is_non_gated_moe = is_moe and self.model.is_non_gated_moe
        self._use_ep = bool(
            vllm_config and vllm_config.parallel_config.enable_expert_parallel
        )
        self._init_punica_wrapper(max_num_batched_tokens, vllm_config)
        self._create_lora_modules()
    
        self._classification_head: tuple[str, ClassificationHeadWithLoRA] | None = None
        if self.is_pooling_model:
            classification_heads = [
                (module_name, module)
                for module_name, module in self.modules.items()
                if isinstance(module, ClassificationHeadWithLoRA)
            ]
            if classification_heads:
                assert len(classification_heads) == 1, (
                    &quot;Expected 1 classification head, but found &quot;
```

```
<source kind="search">
<title>[Bug]: Gemma4 MoE cannot load PEFT expert LoRA adapters without --enable-mixed-moe-lora-format · Issue #64436 · vllm-project/vllm · GitHub</title>
<location>GitHub issue 64436 in vllm-project/vllm (link omitted to avoid creating a cross-reference)</location>
<excerpt>citeturn0search0 [wordlim: 200] Published: today; Crawled: today;       File &quot;.../vllm/lora/model_manager.py&quot;, line 1317, in _validate_moe_lora_format
### 🐛 Describe the bug

Gemma4 MoE checkpoints (for example `google/gemma-4-26B-A4B-it`) store expert weights as fused 3D parameters (`experts.gate_up_proj`, `experts.down_proj`). PEFT can only target them through `target_parameters`, so Gemma4 expert LoRA adapters are always written in the fused 3D layout (`experts.base_layer.lora_{A,B}` for gate_up, `experts.lora_{A,B}` for down).

`Gemma4ForCausalLM` and `Gemma4ForConditionalGeneration` do not set `is_3d_moe_weight`, so vLLM wraps the experts with the 2D `FusedMoEWithLoRA`. Loading such an adapter with the default configuration then fails in `_validate_moe_lora_format`. The error is raised inside EngineCore while activating the adapter, so the whole engine dies (`EngineDeadError`) rather than only that request failing.

The documented workaround (`--enable-mixed-moe-lora-format` plus `is_3d_lora_weight=True` on the request) loads the adapter correctly, but users have to know about it. Other models with fused 3D expert weights (Qwen3.5/Qwen3.6 MoE, Qwen3-VL-MoE, gpt-oss) set `is_3d_moe_weight` and load these adapters by default.
        self.add_adapter(lora)
      File &quot;.../vllm/lora/worker_manager.py&quot;, line 303, in add_adapter
        lora = self._load_adapter(lora_request)
      File &quot;.../vllm/lora/worker_manager.py&quot;, line 156, in _load_adapter
        self._adapter_manager._validate_moe_lora_format(lora)
      File &quot;.../vllm/lora/model_manager.py&quot;, line 1317, in _validate_moe_lora_format
        raise ValueError(
    ValueError: LoRA adapter 1 contains fused 3D MoE weights for &#39;language_model.model.layers.0.experts&#39;, but the model uses a 2D MoE LoRA wrapper. Start the engine with enable_mixed_moe_lora_format=True (--enable-mixed-moe-lora-format) and set is_3d_lora_weight=True on the LoRA request.
    ...
    vllm.v1.engine.exceptions.EngineDeadError: EngineCore encountered an issue. See stack trace (above) for the root cause.
    `

Expected: the adapter loads with the default configuration and the expert deltas are applied, as for other fused 3D MoE models.

Related: Unsloth exports Gemma4 MoE expert adapters in this layout (see the MoE comment in #41754). On older main the expert deltas were silently dropped, which #50252 addressed through a mapper that no longer exists on current main; on current main loading fails as above. #43254 adds `is_3d_moe_weight` to `Gemma4ForCausalLM` only.</excerpt>
</source>

<title>model_manager - vLLM</title>
<location>https://docs.vllm.ai/en/stable/api/vllm/lora/model_manager/</location>
<excerpt>citeturn0search1 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: last week;         def _validate_moe_lora_format(self, lora_model: LoRAModel) -&gt; None: ...                 lora_model, &quot;is_3d_lora_weight&quot;, False ...                     raise ValueError(
                        f&quot;LoRA adapter {lora_model.id} contains fused 3D MoE weights &quot;
                    )
            return lora_model.get_lora(org_module_name)
    
        def _get_module_to_save_weights(
            self, lora_model: LoRAModel, module_name: str
        ) -&gt; LoRAFullModuleWeights | None:
            weights = lora_model.get_module_to_save(module_name)
            if weights is not None:
                return weights
    
            unprefixed_module_name = module_name.removeprefix(&quot;model.&quot;)
            weights = lora_model.get_module_to_save(unprefixed_module_name)
            if weights is not None and unprefixed_module_name != module_name:
                logger.info_once(
                    &quot;For the pool model, successfully loaded the full module weights &quot;
                    &quot;after removing the prefix &#39;model.&#39;.&quot;
                )
            return weights
    
        def _validate_moe_lora_format(self, lora_model: LoRAModel) -&gt; None:
            if self._enable_mixed_moe_lora_format and getattr(
                lora_model, &quot;is_3d_lora_weight&quot;, False
            ):
                return
            for module_name, module in self.modules.items():
                if not isinstance(module, FusedMoEWithLoRA) or isinstance(
                    module, FusedMoE3DWithLoRA
                ):
                    continue
                module_lora = self._get_lora_layer_weights(lora_model, module_name)
                if module_lora is not None and torch.is_tensor(module_lora.lora_a):
                    raise ValueError(
                        f&quot;LoRA adapter {lora_model.id} contains fused 3D MoE weights &quot;
                        f&quot;for {module_name!r}, but the model uses a 2D MoE LoRA &quot;
                        &quot;wrapper. Start the engine with &quot;
                        &quot;enable_mixed_moe_lora_format=True &quot;
                        &quot;(--enable-mixed-moe-lora-format) and set &quot;
                        &quot;is_3d_lora_weight=True on the LoRA request.&quot;
                    )
    
        def _validate_modules_to_save(self, lora_model: LoRAModel) -&gt; None:
            if not lora_model.modules_to_save:
                return
            saved_module_name = next(iter(lora_model.modules_to_save))
            if self._classification_head is None:
                raise ValueError(
                    f&quot;Cannot load full module {saved_module_name!r}: the model does &quot;
                    &quot;not expose a unique ClassificationHeadWithLoRA.&quot;
                )
    
            module_name, wrapper = self._classification_head
            full_module = self._get_module_to_save_weights(lora_model, module_name)
```

**Prioritize the shared-LoRA layout for 3D models.**

When `is_3d_moe=True`, `vllm_enable_mixed_moe_lora_format=False`, and `vllm_enable_moe_shared_loras=True`, vLLM selects its 2D `FusedMoEWithLoRA` path. `get_per_tensor_param()` currently selects `stack_3d_moe` first, which can make adapter loading fail or use the wrong layout.

Check `vllm_shared` before `is_3d_moe`, and update the existing parametrized regression test to expect the shared layout for this combination.

<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>
<dl>
<dd>

```diff
-                if is_3d_moe:
-                    export_kwargs["stack_3d_moe"] = True
-                elif vllm_shared:
+                if vllm_shared:
                     export_kwargs["moe_shared_loras"] = True
+                elif is_3d_moe:
+                    export_kwargs["stack_3d_moe"] = True
                 else:
                     export_kwargs["expand_shared_outer"] = True
```

```diff
-    expected = "stack_3d_moe" if is_3d and not mixed else "moe_shared_loras" if shared else "expand_shared_outer"
+    expected = "moe_shared_loras" if shared else "stack_3d_moe" if is_3d and not mixed else "expand_shared_outer"
```

</dd>
</dl>
</details>

**<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/major-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Major** · **Data Integrity & Integration** · **Quick win**

**Save a PEFT-loadable adapter by default.**

With shared-outer LoRA, `allow_serving_layout=True` saves the shared factor in a serving-only layout. Bridge documents that `PeftModel.from_pretrained` cannot load this layout. Use `expand_shared_outer=True` for the HF adapter checkpoint, or save the serving layout separately. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/Megatron-Bridge/main/src/megatron/bridge/models/conversion/auto_bridge.py))

**Reject nonzero `lr_warmup_init` when LoRA+ is enabled, or give the B group its own warmup start.**

When `lr_warmup_init > 0` and `lora_plus_ratio != 1.0`, the A and B learning rates start at the same value. Megatron-Core computes warmup from one shared `init_lr`. Bridge's LoRA+ overrides do not change that value. The B-to-A ratio is therefore `1` at the first warmup step and reaches `lora_plus_ratio` only when warmup ends. The default `lr_warmup_init: 0.0` is not affected.

Options:
- Raise a configuration error in `get_megatron_optimizer` or the caller when both `lora_plus_ratio != 1.0` and `lr_warmup_init > 0` are set.
- Or add a separate warmup path that sets a per-group initial rate. This needs a Megatron-Core change, because the overrides do not carry an initial rate.

**Pass the rollout backend to the exporter.**

For an SGLang rollout with shared-outer LoRA, the exporter still selects a vLLM-specific layout because it has no backend information. SGLang receives that layout instead of the default shared-outer layout it expects. Pass the backend to the engine and retain the default Bridge export for SGLang. **Normalize vLLM option names before reading them.**

`build_cli_args_from_config` emits hyphenated keys as CLI options, so vLLM honors `enable-mixed-moe-lora-format` and `enable-moe-shared-loras`. The actor-side lookups still read only underscore-form keys and therefore default to `False`. The actor and vLLM can select different MoE LoRA layouts.


**Reject zero before the optimizer disables LoRA+.**

`hf_model.yaml` defines `lr_B = lora_plus_ratio * lr_A`, so `lora_plus_ratio=0` requests a zero B learning rate. `get_megatron_optimizer` instead skips the Bridge overrides for zero, so it cannot apply that ratio and leaves B on the ordinary optimizer path.

<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>
<dl>
<dd>

```diff
--- "a/verl/workers/engine/megatron/transformer_impl.py"
+++ "b/verl/workers/engine/megatron/transformer_impl.py"
@@ -497,8 +497,12 @@
         )
         # LoRA+: lr_B = lora_plus_ratio * lr_A (read from the lora config dict).
         lora_cfg = getattr(self.model_config, "lora", {})
         lora_plus_ratio = float(lora_cfg.get("lora_plus_ratio", 1.0))
-        if lora_plus_ratio not in (0.0, 1.0) and self.optimizer_config.lr_warmup_init > 0:
+        if lora_plus_ratio == 0.0:
+            raise ValueError(
+                "model.lora.lora_plus_ratio must be positive; use 1.0 to disable LoRA+."
+            )
+        if lora_plus_ratio != 1.0 and self.optimizer_config.lr_warmup_init > 0:
             raise ValueError(
                 "model.lora.lora_plus_ratio requires optim.lr_warmup_init=0: Megatron warms every param group up "
                 "from one shared init lr, so the LoRA+ B/A ratio would not hold during warmup."
```

</dd>
</dl>
</details>

- HollowMan6 · comment · 2026-11-03T23:16:26Z · [source](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7484#discussion_r4461606947)

File: `verl/utils/checkpoint/megatron_checkpoint_manager.py` · line 993

The checkpoint path now uses `expand_shared_outer=True` only for an explicitly enabled shared-outer adapter. It no longer passes `allow_serving_layout`, and ordinary checkpoints pass neither keyword. The expanded checkpoint is PEFT-loadable. This opt-in requires a Bridge save API that supports `expand_shared_outer`, which is present in v0.7.0 and absent in v0.6.2. The [Megatron LoRA guide](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/blob/6971bc099711b36585970d1bceed8a5034854acb/docs/advance/ppo_lora.rst#megatron-backend-usage-guide) now records that requirement.


- HollowMan6 · comment · 2026-11-03T23:16:28Z · [source](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7484#discussion_r4461607168)

File: `verl/utils/megatron_utils.py` · line None

Fixed the legacy fused-gradient path in 9157f241. Bridge 0.6.2 now keeps fusion on the base model and disables it only for trainable EP-replicated LoRA projections. Its existing EP hooks receive the true autograd gradients, and native DDP accumulates them into main_grad. Builds with both expert-adapter helpers keep the native Bridge finalizer.

Both CPU stacks passed 19 compatibility tests. A two-rank Gloo probe using the pinned Bridge and MCore, the actual verl helper, native linears and the native DDP backward hook matched every element of both main_grad buffers against the eager reference after each of two microbatches, for FC1 and FC2.

Legacy shared-outer initialization and gradient hooks use the entire pipeline-stage group. In eda09da3, frozen unsharded replicas are allowed; trainable factors and actual TP/GTP shards still require identical stage and EP membership. The modern Bridge path is unchanged.

GPU validation: [the exact expert-parallel CI job](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/actions/runs/41762050376/job/126352233982) passed on eda09da3 (attempt 1), including the previously failing LoRA GSM8K case on 8×L20. The fused-training and FP8-rollout prerequisites and all job steps also succeeded.

**<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/major-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Major** · **Stability & Availability** · **Quick win**

**Allow frozen shared weights when the groups differ.**

If the shared weight is frozen, this check still raises `RuntimeError` when the pipeline-stage and EP rank sets differ. Bridge’s backward hook has no shared-weight gradient to reduce in that case. This blocks shared-outer training with a frozen shared factor, including the EP=1, data-parallel layout covered by `test_legacy_bridge_shared_outer_group`. Select the shared weight before this check. Require matching rank sets only when that weight is trainable. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/Megatron-Bridge/v0.6.2/src/megatron/bridge/peft/utils.py))

- HollowMan6 · comment · 2026-11-04T02:39:35Z · [source](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7484#discussion_r4462945893)

File: `verl/utils/megatron_utils.py` · line 1074

Fixed the frozen-replica case in eda09da3. The legacy path now allows a frozen shared projection when its actual TP group and GTP rematerialization shard count are both unsharded.

The initialization guard still applies to actual shards: Bridge 0.6.2 broadcasts the shared weight across the stage during construction, before the backward hook is relevant. Freezing a TP/GTP shard does not make that broadcast safe.

Both CPU stacks passed 19 tests. A two-rank Gloo probe constructed the real Bridge SharedOuter adapters for FC1 and FC2: ordinary TP=2/ETP=1 full frozen replicas were accepted, and ordinary TP=1/ETP=2 frozen shards were rejected. GTP shard rejection is covered by the boundary tests and the pinned MCore construction code; that probe used GTP=1.

- coderabbitai[bot] · comment · 2026-11-04T02:56:15Z · [source](https://github.com/verl-project/verl/pull/7484#discussion_r4463048756)

File: `verl/utils/megatron_utils.py` · line 1074

<!-- coderabbit-ai-generated:v1 -->

@HollowMan6 The supplied code addresses the frozen-replica case. It selects the shared projection first and bypasses the rank-set guard only when the weight is frozen, its actual TP group is unsharded, and `gtp_remat_size == 1`.

My original recommendation was too broad for frozen shards. Keeping the initialization guard for TP/GTP shards is correct because freezing does not make the stage-wide broadcast safe.

The reported tests cover the replica/shard distinction. I have not rerun them.
